Checked on load: the digest equals `compute_snapshot_digest` of its JSON document (`DigestMismatch`, `DigestKeyUnavailable`, `UnknownDigestScheme` unwrapped); the persona, every evaluator binding reference and every binding's definition resolve to an embedded record of that `(id, version)` (`ValueError` otherwise) and a digest on such a reference is verified (`RefDigestMismatchError`, E5); every binding belongs to the evaluator; `rubric.validate_rubric` passes (bindings and definitions of the snapshot); the evaluator has at least one expected-outcome check (an enabled binding of an `llm_judge`, boolean, per_conversation, `equals_true` definition that lists `expected_outcome` in `prompt_variables`) and every such check is a required rubric item |
| `snapshot_digest_domain(doc) -> dict` | function (P) | on the JSON document: the generic domain (no top-level `content_digest` and `schema_version`, no null members at any depth) minus the `schema_version` of `evaluator`, `persona`, `rubric` and of each `metric_definitions[]` and `metric_bindings[]` entry, minus `evaluator.test_profile.content_digest`; embedded `content_digest` values stay; a `schema_version` key inside user `metadata` is not removed. Since revision 11 (N20) the embedded `evaluator.content_digest` that stays in the domain is itself computed without `test_profile.content_digest`, so dropping or keying the profile digest changes neither digest |
| `compute_snapshot_digest(doc) -> ContentDigest` | function (P) | plain `sha256-jcs/v1` over the JCS bytes of the domain |
| `check_internal_snapshot(snapshot) -> None` | function (P) | `ValueError` unless `evaluator.test_profile` is absent or carries a plain digest |
| `check_pricing_catalog_ref(ref, catalog_doc) -> None` | function (P) | `catalog_doc` is a JSON mapping with `id`, `version`, `content_digest`. Compares `ref.content_digest` (N26). `ValueError` for another ID or version, or a digest that cannot be compared (another scheme, or keyed with another `key_ref`); `RefDigestMismatchError` for equal scheme and `key_ref` and different value; two keyed digests with the same `key_ref` are compared (ADR 0009 Decision 7) |
| `lint_profile_values_in_free_text(snapshot, profile) -> list[IngestWarning]` | function | one warning per `find_literal_profile_values(snapshot, profile)` hit, code `profile_value_in_free_text`, `path` the RFC 6901 pointer from the snapshot document root, `raw` absent, a message without the value; sorted like the hits |

**`tracer_evalschema.run` (P1-12)**

| Name | Kind | Pinned contract |
|---|---|---|
| `AgentTargetRef` | value object (in `agent`, P, N35) | `VersionedRef` narrowed to `AgentTargetId` |
| `CallerExternalId` | value object | `value: VerbatimText` (non-empty), `privacy: PrivacyMarker` (required); hides its input in errors |
| `Run` (`run`) | entity | `project_id: ProjectId`, `agent_target: AgentTargetRef`, `config_snapshot_id: ConfigSnapshotId` (P, N35), `mode` (simulated, recorded), `caller_origin` (simulated, human; required, no default), `started_at?: UtcDatetime` (no default, never the ingest time), `caller_external_id?`, `detected_capabilities: list[InputKind]` (unique, default `[]`), `channel_maps: list[ChannelMap]` (default `[]`), `available_inputs: list[InputKind]` (unique, default `[]`), `observed_tier` (T0, T1, T2; closed, required, no default; rev 12, O3 decided by the owner, option A: the highest tier whose evidence the run holds, display and filter only; rule R1, rev 13, O3a decided by the owner, option A: `spans` in `available_inputs` with `T0` is rejected, error at `observed_tier`, `detected_capabilities` not read; ADR 0004 Decision 2a; rule R2 is a bundle check at P1-14), `external_refs: list[ExternalRef]` (default `[]`), `ingest_warnings: list[IngestWarning]` (default `[]`). `mode=simulated` with `caller_origin=human` is rejected (error at `caller_origin`); the `agent_audio` rule above (at `available_inputs`). `hide_input_in_errors=True` (ADR 0007 Decision 8 (b)) |

**`tracer_evalschema.dataset` (P1-13)**

| Name | Kind | Pinned contract |
|---|---|---|
| `Dataset` (`dts`) | versioned entity | `name: HumanLabel`, `item_ids: list[DatasetItemId]` (unique, ordered, may be empty; N25, decided by the owner in revision 11: membership is inside the version's digest, so adding or removing an item is a new Dataset version) |
| `DatasetItem` (`dti`) | entity | no `dataset_id` (N25); `language: SupportedLanguage` (required), `artifact_id?: ArtifactId` (a recording), `transcript_id?: TranscriptId`, `source_run_id?: RunId`, `privacy?: PrivacyMarker` required (error at `privacy`) when `artifact_id` or `transcript_id` is present. With a payload and no `source_run_id` a marker with `human_subject=false` is rejected (ADR 0016 Decision 5: no role evidence derives true). No `declared_language`, no mixed-language field. (rev 12, O7 decided by the owner, option A) `source_run_id` is provenance only, never required to resolve; exports treat it as an unresolved reference (ADR 0016 Decision 9). (rev 13, O9 decided by the owner, option A) `internal` exports keep it; `shared_export` and `benchmark` exports keep it only when the run it names is in the same bundle, and otherwise omit it from the exported copy and record the omission as a redaction (P1-14; ADR 0016 Decision 9) |

### 22.2 Test map

Every SCOPE section 5 name of these tasks exists once (the revision 5 to 10 names included): P1-10a (10 names, plus `test_artifact_uri_rejects_presigned_signature`, which SCOPE lists under P1-06 and which waited for `Artifact`), P1-10b (21), P1-10c (11), P1-11 (all, including the P-58, P-59, P-61, N14 and P-62 names, plus `test_wer_not_applicable_for_observed_romanized` and `test_romanized_observed_scored_when_not_script_sensitive`, which section 14 deferred), P1-12 (15), P1-13 (3), P1-22 (all that belong to `RunVerdict`; the rubric names of that row exist in `test_rubric.py` since batch 2), P1-27 (17). `test_policy_documents_free_text_lint_limitation` stays red until P1-20a writes `SCHEMA_POLICY.md`. The ExportBundle halves of `test_user_id_not_in_snapshot_or_export` and `test_evidence_ref_resolves_in_bundle`, and every P1-14 test, are not written: they need `RunBundle` and `ExportBundle`.

Proposed names (the docstring says so): `test_run_verdict_shape_and_enums`, `test_empty_verdict_rejected`, `test_weighted_exclusions_are_unique_per_binding`, `test_outcome_of_result_needs_a_pass_decision`, `test_verdict_check_metric_ref_is_typed_and_equals_binding_metric` (named in SCOPE as "proposed" in revision 8), `test_weighted_score_checked_against_rubric_and_results`, `test_metric_result_shape_and_enums`, `test_metric_result_evidence_refs_are_evidence_ref_objects`, `test_judge_info_shape`, `test_judge_info_present_only_on_scored_or_errored_judge_results`, `test_check_result_against_definition_checks_the_reference`, `test_effective_language_steps_table`, `test_untagged_import_worked_example_6f`, `test_untagged_import_with_declaration_worked_example_6h`, `test_privacy_marker_shape_and_reason_set`, `test_channel_role_set_exact`, `test_channel_map_indexes_unique`, `test_artifact_errors_do_not_echo_uri_credentials`, `test_exchange_shape_and_sources`, `test_turn_and_segment_bidi_flag_computed_and_verified`, `test_transcript_shape_and_optional_fields`, `test_tool_call_event_shape`, `test_stage_span_shape`, `test_evidence_ref_shape`, `test_snapshot_shape`, `test_snapshot_validates_the_rubric_against_bindings`, `test_run_shape_and_enums`, `test_run_detected_capabilities_separate_from_declared`, `test_run_channel_maps_and_warnings_are_value_objects`, `test_dataset_item_recording_without_run_is_human_subject`, `test_dataset_is_a_versioned_entity`, and in `test_batch3_entities.py` (parametrized over the twelve entities) `test_batch3_entity_is_registered_with_its_prefix`, `..._roundtrip_and_schema_agree`, `..._rejects_unknown_fields`, `..._checks_schema_version_and_id_kind`, `test_batch3_digested_entity_digest_is_verified`, `test_batch3_records_of_fact_are_not_versioned`, `test_batch3_models_emit_enum_not_const`, `test_batch3_models_are_all_found_by_the_registry_walk`.

### 22.3 Ambiguities and contradictions (listed, not guessed; the pinned reading is in 22.1)

Numbering continues the architect's N-series (last used: N19).

**Revision 11 status (schema-architect, 2026-10-23).** The owner decided N20 to N26 in chat (one at a time; recorded in the decision log under "Batch 3 decisions"). N27 has a default applied that the owner may revisit. The architect resolved N28 (in part), N29, N31 (in part), N32 to N35, N37, N39 and N40, where the ADRs and the core rules give one answer; each entry says why. N28 (`ToolCallEvent.source`), N30, N31 (`ConfigSnapshot`) and N36 are genuine design choices and are listed for the owner in section 22.10. N38 goes to staff-reviewer with N12. No test was edited by this revision; section 22.9 lists the test changes for qa.

- **N20 (contradiction, ADR 0006 Decision 5 against ADR 0009 Decision 3).** The snapshot digest domain excludes `evaluator.test_profile.content_digest` so that a shared export can drop or key the profile digest and the snapshot digest still verifies. But the embedded `Evaluator` is a `VersionedEntity` whose own `content_digest` covers its `test_profile.content_digest` and is verified on load, and that digest stays inside the snapshot domain. A document with the profile digest dropped or keyed therefore fails the embedded evaluator's own check (and `compute_snapshot_digest` would differ through the evaluator's digest). Either the Evaluator's digest domain must also exclude `test_profile.content_digest` (an entity-specific exclusion, ADR 0006 Decision 5 depth), or the ref must live outside the evaluator. The P1-12 tests only exercise the function on the JSON document (the evaluator's digest is left as it is when the reference digest is edited), so they hold under either answer; `test_shared_export_snapshot_digest_verifies_after_profile_digest_dropped` (P1-14) will hit it. Needs staff-reviewer or owner.

  **Decided (revision 11, owner): option A.** The Evaluator's digest domain also excludes `test_profile.content_digest`, an entity-specific exclusion at that one path (ADR 0006 Decision 5, ADR 0009 Decision 3). Both digests (the evaluator's and the snapshot's) survive dropping or keying the profile digest. The evaluator digest no longer proves which profile content was used; `test_profile.id` and `version` still pin the profile version, and the content is pinned by the reference's own digest in internal data and by the TestProfile's own verified digest. Rejected: moving the reference out of the evaluator (B), keyed-only shared exports (C). New function `evaluator.evaluator_digest_domain(doc)` (22.1). The P1-12 tests now hold for the right reason; the batch 2 and batch 3 helpers that seal an Evaluator with a profile digest must use the new domain (22.9).
- **N21 (missing link, ADR 0011).** `Turn` and `Transcript` have no field that ties a turn to its transcript, yet `seq` is "unique within its transcript", `timeline_origin` is "required iff any of its turns have offsets" and the language steps read a turn's transcript. Pinned: `Turn.transcript_id: TranscriptId` and the function `check_transcript`. Alternative: `Transcript` embeds `turns` (then 50,000 `Turn` entities sit inside one record and EvidenceRef points into it).

  **Decided (revision 11, owner): option A**, as pinned. `Turn.transcript_id` plus `check_transcript`; a Transcript does not embed its turns. ADR 0011 Decisions 1 and 3 updated.
- **N22 (missing links, ADR 0001 table).** Except for `UsageRecord.run_id`, no record of fact names its run (Transcript, Artifact, ToolCallEvent, StageSpan, Exchange, MetricResult, RunVerdict), so "unique per run" and "of the same run" are checks over the records of one bundle (`check_tool_call_seqs(events)`, `check_transcript(..., exchanges)`). Pinned: no `run_id` on them. P1-14 must define the bundle as the unit.

  **Decided (revision 11, owner): option A.** No `run_id` on records of fact except `UsageRecord`; `RunBundle` is the unit of a run; cross-record rules ("unique per run", "of the same run") are bundle-level `check_*` functions, which P1-14 calls over the records of one bundle. ADR 0001 table, ADR 0009 Decision 5, ADR 0011 Decision 4 and ADR 0012 Decision 1 updated. `DatasetItem.source_run_id` is provenance of a dataset item, not membership of a run; it is kept as pinned (owner confirmation asked in 22.10, item O7). **Revision 12: O7 decided by the owner (option A):** kept as optional provenance only; exports treat it as an unresolved reference kept only where policy allows (ADR 0016 Decision 9; the policy is O9, 22.11). **Revision 13: O9 decided by the owner (option A):** `internal` keeps it; `shared_export` and `benchmark` keep it only when the source run is in the same bundle, otherwise it is dropped from the exported copy with a recorded redaction.
- **N23 (contradiction inside ADR 0004).** Decision 3 makes `agent_audio` missing from `available_inputs` a `missing_input`, Decision 5 makes the same situation `agent_channel_unknown`, and the precedence puts `missing_input` first, so `agent_channel_unknown` could never be reported. `test_deg_agent_audio_na_when_channel_unknown` (P1-19) needs it to be reportable. Pinned (P): `agent_channel_unknown` when `agent_audio` is the only missing kind, `audio` is available and no channel map identifies the agent; any other missing kind gives `missing_input` with all missing kinds listed. Not pinned: `agent_audio` missing without `audio`, and `agent_audio` missing together with other kinds. The tests check only the first case.

  **Decided (revision 11, owner): option A.** `agent_channel_unknown` only when `agent_audio` is the only missing kind, `audio` is available and no channel map identifies the agent; every other case is `missing_input` listing all missing kinds. Worked examples for the unpinned combinations (no recording; `agent_audio` with other kinds; a map that identifies the agent while `agent_audio` is not listed) are in ADR 0004 Decision 5a. qa adds a test (22.9).
- **N24 (signature, SCOPE P1-27 and ADR 0016 Decision 5).** `derive_human_subject(run)` reads "any Turn role in any transcript", which a `Run` does not hold. Pinned: `derive_human_subject(run, turns=())` and `check_human_subject_marker(marker, run, turns=())`. The same applies to P1-14's `export_human_subject(marker, run)`.

  **Decided (revision 11, owner): option A.** `derive_human_subject(run, turns)`, `check_human_subject_marker(marker, run, turns)` and `export_human_subject(marker, run, turns)`. No turns supplied means no turn role evidence; with no channel role either, the result is true, never false. ADR 0016 Decisions 5 and 7a and SCOPE P1-27 updated. Open detail: whether `turns` may be omitted (default `()`, as the tests call it) or must be passed explicitly (22.10, item O1). **Revision 12: O1 decided by the owner (option A):** `turns` is required in all three functions; omitting it is a `TypeError` (22.9 item 12).
- **N25 (reversed reference, ADR 0001 table).** `dts` is "referenced by DatasetItem" and `dti` "referenced by Dataset". Both cannot hold. Pinned: a `Dataset` version lists its `item_ids` (so membership is inside the digest, which immutability needs) and `DatasetItem` has no `dataset_id`. `DatasetItem`'s payload fields (`artifact_id`, `transcript_id`, `source_run_id`, `privacy`) are not named in SCOPE either.

  **Decided (revision 11, owner): option A**, as pinned. ADR 0001's table is fixed (`dti` is referenced by `Dataset.item_ids`; `dts` by nothing in 0.0.1), ADR 0009 Decision 5 states that membership is part of a Dataset version, and SCOPE's entity specs name the DatasetItem fields.
- **N26 (field name, ADR 0006 against ADR 0009).** `PricingCatalogRef` has `digest`; the other references and `bundle.check_bundle_consistency` use `content_digest`, so the bundle seam would not see a catalog reference's digest. Pinned: the field is `digest` and `snapshot.check_pricing_catalog_ref` verifies it; P1-14 either teaches the seam both names or renames the field (a rename changes ADR 0006).

  **Decided (revision 11, owner): rename.** `PricingCatalogRef.content_digest` (required). ADR 0006 Decision 1 and I16, ADR 0009 Decision 4 and SCOPE updated; the bundle seam sees the catalog reference's digest like every other reference's. `test_pricing_catalog_ref_digest_verified` must change (22.9). Follow-on question: `CostRecord.pricing_snapshot.digest` (ADR 0014) has the same name problem (22.10, item O6). **Revision 12: O6 decided by the owner (option A):** renamed `content_digest` (ADR 0014 Decision 12; 22.9 item 18).
- **N27 (no-fabrication, ADR 0012).** `AudioFormat.bits_per_sample` is listed without a question mark, but a lossy codec (Opus, MP3) has no bit depth, and the core rule forbids inventing one. The tests give it in every document and do not test its absence. Decide whether it is optional.

  **Default applied (revision 11; owner did not object, may revisit): optional.** `AudioFormat.bits_per_sample?`; a producer omits it when the source states none, and nothing ties it to a codec. ADR 0012 Decision 4 and SCOPE P1-10a updated. `test_audio_format_fields` must change (it rejects `None` for this field) and a test of the absence is added (22.9).
- **N28 (types not named).** `ToolCallEvent.source` (the ADRs give no set; the tests use `agent_telemetry`, valid as a MachineKey and as the transcript producer set), `StageSpan.span_ref` (pinned as `ExternalRef` because ADR 0012 Decision 6 lists trace IDs as `ExternalRef`s), `IngestWarning.path` (text; no pointer syntax), `Transcript.provider_call_ref` (not tested) and `ToolCallEvent.external_call_id` (verbatim text). The "truncated payload never yields `arguments_complete`" acceptance is pinned as: `arguments_complete=true` with unparseable or non-object `arguments_raw` is rejected.

  **Revision 11.** Resolved by the architect, part by part:
  - `StageSpan.span_ref: ExternalRef`, required: ADR 0012 Decision 2 lists it without `?` and Decision 6 makes trace and span IDs `ExternalRef`s.
  - `IngestWarning.path: VerbatimText?`: the path locates a value in its *source*, whose format differs per mapping (a JSON pointer into an OTLP payload, a line number of an F8 file). Imposing RFC 6901 would force a guessed translation; each mapping's field-provenance table (P-51) states its path form.
  - `Transcript.provider_call_ref: ExternalRef?`: a provider's request identifier is a foreign identifier, which ADR 0012 Decision 6 records as an `ExternalRef`, never as text or as our ID. The same type applies to `UsageRecord.provider_call_ref` (P1-23).
  - `ToolCallEvent.external_call_id: VerbatimText?`: ADR 0001 I12 lists it as an opaque field stored verbatim.
  - `arguments_complete`: the pinned reading stands. ADR 0012 Decision 1 says `arguments` is a parsed JSON object and `arguments_complete` is never inferred true from a payload detected as truncated; parse failure is the only truncation the schema can detect, so `true` with a raw that is not a whole JSON object is rejected. A complete payload that is legitimately not a JSON object is a mapping gap under ADR 0018, recorded with an `IngestWarning`, never a guessed flag; tool metrics treat it like an incomplete payload (ADR 0012 Consequences).
  - Not resolved in revision 11: whether `ToolCallEvent.source` is a closed set (22.10, item O2). **Revision 12: O2 decided by the owner (option A):** closed `agent_export | agent_telemetry | observer` (ADR 0003 Decision 1; 22.9 item 13).
- **N29 (open edge, ADR 0004 Decision 6g).** For a language-dependent metric with no effective language on any read turn, `language_source` is None; whether `language_not_observed` results that also had some tagged turn must record `observed` is "either" in the tests (the function reports `observed`). A call to `check_language` with no turn and no transcript is unspecified and untested.

  **Resolved (revision 11, architect).** (a) ADR 0004 Decision 6g makes `language_source` *required* for `language_not_observed` when some other read turn had an effective language, so the record is not free; `check_language` already reports it, `check_result_against_definition` cannot verify it because it has no turns, and the P1-14 bundle check can (by re-running `check_language` over the bundle's turns). (b) With no turn and no transcript, a language-dependent definition has no tag to read, so Decision 6d gives `language_not_observed` with `language_source=None` and no IDs; a language-independent one gives `(None, None, [])`. qa adds a test (22.9).
- **N30 (set not in ADR 0003).** `Run.observed_tier` is a display summary; the tests accept `T0`, `T1`, `T2`, reject other spellings (P) and require the field (SCOPE lists it without the question mark that marks `started_at`, `caller_external_id` and `detected_capabilities` as optional). ADR 0003 Decision 1 does not list it as a closed set.

  **Revision 11: needs owner decision** (22.10, item O3). The ADRs fix only that no logic reads it (ADR 0004 Decision 2); its member set, whether it is closed, whether it is required and what a run with both telemetry and observer evidence shows are not stated anywhere.

  **Revision 12: O3 decided by the owner (option A):** closed and required `T0 | T1 | T2`, the highest tier whose evidence the run holds (so a run with both telemetry and observer evidence shows `T2`), display and filter only, and a check rejects values contradicting recorded capabilities. The contradiction rules are open for owner confirmation (O3a, 22.11; ADR 0004 Decision 2a). **Revision 13: O3a decided by the owner (option A):** R1 (Run record, P1-12) and R2 (bundle check, P1-14) are accepted; P1-14 must define how a bundle declares records deliberately withheld, so that R2 does not reject a legitimate partial export (ADR 0004 Decision 2a; constraints in 22.12).
- **N31 (scope of the no-echo rule, ADR 0007 Decision 8).** Pinned: `Artifact` (named in Decision 8 (a)), `Run` and `CallerExternalId` (caller phone numbers) hide their input, and the set stays closed under embedding. Not pinned: `Transcript`, `Turn`, `ToolCallEvent` and `ConfigSnapshot`, which hold free text that can be personal data (ADR 0006 Consequences names the snapshot). Decision 8 (b) says "other PII-bearing models at their tasks" without a list.

  **Revision 11: resolved in part (architect).** `Transcript`, `Turn`, `Segment`, `ToolCallEvent` and `IngestWarning` hide their input. Reason: they hold the text and tool-argument payloads that ADR 0016 Decision 7 treats as personal data on a human call, and IngestWarning's `raw` holds source values verbatim, so Decision 8 (b) applies to them directly (Segment and IngestWarning also because they are validated on their own as registered value objects). `ConfigSnapshot` is different: by ADR 0006 it holds no personal data by design (placeholders, a zero `x-pii` walk), and only user-typed text that the lint may miss could carry some; whether that is enough to hide its input, and the input of the embedded `Evaluator`, is a design choice (22.10, item O4). ADR 0007 Decision 8 updated. **Revision 12: O4 decided by the owner (option A):** `ConfigSnapshot` and `Evaluator` hide their input; the definition models validated on their own are N41 (22.11). **Revision 13: N41 decided by the owner (option A):** `ScriptedTurn`, `ExpectedToolCall`, `Persona`, `MetricDefinition`, `MetricBinding` and `Rubric` hide their input too, closed under embedding.
- **N32 (P-62 "flagged").** The P-62 test text says bidi controls in `reported_model` are "flagged", but a plain `VerbatimText` field has no flag. The test checks that such a value is stored unchanged and that `contains_bidi_controls()` detects it.

  **Resolved (revision 11, architect).** The owner accepted P-62 with the type `VerbatimText?` (ADR 0010 Decision 6), and every other raw provider string (`detected_language_raw`, `Turn.language_raw`, `arguments_raw`) is a plain `VerbatimText` whose bidi controls are detected by `contains_bidi_controls()` and shown inert by `display_safe()` (ADR 0017 Decisions 1 to 3). A stored flag (`BidiFlaggedText`) is used only for the main text of a turn or segment. "Flagged" therefore means detected; the test's reading stands.
- **N33 (result to binding).** A `MetricResult` names its definition (`metric`) but not its binding, run or evaluator. A verdict check matches a result to a binding through the binding's `metric` reference; a non-required weighted binding is matched the same way and needs exactly one result per metric in the supplied results (a rescored run would hold two).

  **Resolved (revision 11, architect).** A definition is bound at most once per evaluator (batch 2: definition `id` and key unique per evaluator, `validate_rubric`), and a run has one evaluator (through its snapshot), so a metric reference names exactly one binding of the run; a `binding_id` on the result would only repeat it and could disagree. When the supplied results hold two results of one metric, `check_verdict` rejects rather than choosing one (never assume). Whether a RunBundle may carry a rescoring next to the original results is a P1-14 question (noted in 22.10 under "carried forward").
- **N34 (name drift).** SCOPE lists `test_definition_refs_digest_optional_and_verified_when_present` under P1-09 and P1-12; batch 2 wrote the same check as `test_reference_digest_is_optional_and_verified_when_present` (`test_batch2_entities.py`). Batch 3 adds the SCOPE-named test for the snapshot half. The batch 2 name is kept (no rename without staff-reviewer).

  **Resolved (revision 11, architect).** The SCOPE name is binding (gate item 3) and is listed under both P1-09 and P1-12, so the SCOPE-named test must cover both halves: definition references outside a snapshot (`PersonaRef`, `MetricDefinitionRef`, `MetricBindingRef` on Evaluator, MetricBinding and RubricItem) and the snapshot's references. The batch 2 name may stay as an extra test; it is neither renamed nor withdrawn. qa confirms the coverage (22.9).
- **N35 (references not listed).** ADR 0001's table says `agt` and `evl` are referenced by Run. `AgentTarget` is versioned, so ADR 0009 Decision 4 requires a typed reference object: `AgentTargetRef` is a new value object (the N6 rule covers it). No `EvaluatorRef` is added because the snapshot embeds the evaluator; `Run.project_id`, `Run.agent_target` and `Run.config_snapshot_id` are pinned without a source in SCOPE.

  **Resolved (revision 11, architect).** The three fields follow from ADR 0001's table (`prj` owns everything, `agt` and `snap` are referenced by Run) and ADR 0009 Decision 4 (a versioned target needs a typed reference). A Run names its evaluator only through its snapshot, which embeds the evaluator (ADR 0006 Decision 1); a second reference could disagree with the embedded one. ADR 0001's table now says so, and SCOPE's Run spec lists the three fields.
- **N36 (artifact URI scheme set).** ADR 0007 Decision 6 says `Artifact.uri` follows the endpoint rules but names no schemes. The tests accept `http`, `https`, `s3`, `gs` with a host. Not pinned: `file:` URIs, a fragment, an empty channel list.

  **Revision 11.** Empty channel list, resolved (architect): `ChannelMap.channels` is non-empty. A map with no channel asserts nothing, and read literally ("no channel map" means no role evidence, ADR 0016 Decision 5) an empty map on a simulated run would count as evidence with no `unknown` role and derive false, which is a silent "not human". Role evidence is the set of roles found (ADR 0016 Decision 5 as amended), and an empty list is rejected. The scheme set, `file:` URIs and fragments need an owner decision (22.10, item O5). **Revision 12: O5 decided by the owner (option A):** ADR 0007 Decision 6c; loopback `http`/`https` hosts are O8 (22.11). **Revision 13:** the owner confirmed the O5 details (no query, no dot segments, no empty path, `file:/path` rejected, `file:///C:/...` accepted) and decided O8 (option A): loopback hosts are accepted in records and rejected in shared and benchmark exports (ADR 0007 Decision 6d).
- **N37 (small readings).** `RunVerdict.required_total >= 1` (an empty verdict would be a vacuous `pass`); a scored result with `passed=None` has no verdict outcome; `judge` is required for a scored `llm_judge` result; `MetricResult` forbids `scorer` and `judge` on not_applicable results. Each is pinned by a test named in 22.2.

  **Resolved (revision 11, architect).** Each reading follows from accepted text: a rubric has at least one required item (ADR 0013 Decision 7), so a verdict has `required_total >= 1`; a required item has a pass rule (Decision 7), so its scored result always has `passed`, and a scored result with `passed=None` reaching `outcome_of_result` is an error, not a guessed outcome; P-61 says llm_judge results carry `JudgeInfo` and no `scorer`, and a not_applicable result ran no scorer or judge, so it records neither. ADR 0013 Decision 3 updated.
- **N38 (code-only rules).** JSON Schema cannot state: the URL name and pattern rules, the cross-record rules of the `check_*` functions, snapshot reference resolution and digest verification, `PerTurnSummary` consistency, the `ChannelMap` confidence-by-determination and agent-count rules unless emitted as `if`/`then`, the Turn timing rules, `MetricResult` presence rules, `RunVerdict` count and status rules. Each needs a `KNOWN_SCHEMA_GAPS`-style entry (N12). Tests assert JSON Schema rejection only for closed sets, required fields and patterns.

  **Revision 11: carried to staff-reviewer with N12** (not an owner design question). Recommendation: emit draft 2020-12 `if`/`then` for every presence rule keyed on a closed discriminator (`ChannelMap.determination` and `confidence`; `Turn.timing_source=none` and the offsets; `MetricResult.status` and its presence rules; `StageSpan.stage=other` and `stage_raw`), so pydantic and JSON Schema agree there, and list in `KNOWN_SCHEMA_GAPS` only the rules that need arithmetic, cross-record data, digests or the URL patterns.
- **N39 (JSON union trap).** `ArtifactId | ExternalRef | None` in `TimelineOrigin.ref` serialized the object member through the ID classes' string serializer in the oracle until the union order was changed to `ExternalRef | ArtifactId`. The tests pin the JSON forms; the architect should check the union serialization.

  **Resolved (revision 11, architect).** ADR 0002 Decision 3 names one field, `ref`, holding an `art_` ID or an `ExternalRef`, so the field stays one union. The implementation picks the member by an explicit discriminator on the input's JSON type (string or object) rather than by union order, and the JSON Schema is a `oneOf` of the ID pattern and the `ExternalRef` object. The JSON forms the tests pin are unchanged.
- **N40 (T-DRIFT).** The binding names `test_unsupported_language_na_for_observed_bn_IN` (in `test_results_language.py`) and `test_dataset_item_no_codemix` (in `test_dataset.py`) contain matched tokens, so both modules need an entry in the ADR 0005 Decision 10 allowlist by path. All other tags in the batch 3 files are assembled from parts (`EXCLUDED_LANGUAGE_TAGS`, `OTHER_TAG`, `OTHER_LABEL_RAW`).

  **Resolved (revision 11, architect).** Both modules qualify under the binding-name rule of ADR 0005 Decision 10 (revision 8 already announced the P1-13 module). ADR 0005 Decision 10 now lists `packages/evalschema/tests/test_results_language.py` and `packages/evalschema/tests/test_dataset.py`; the T-DRIFT test's literal allowlist must carry the same two paths when it is written (22.9).

### 22.4 Changes to existing tests (all strengthen; none removed)

- `test_ids.py`: `VALUE_OBJECT_NAMES` gains `ScorerInfo`, `LanguageDeclaration` (section 21.4) and `ChannelAssignment`, `ResultError`, `JudgeTurnVerdict`, `WeightedExclusion`; `EXPECTED_TYPED_REFS` and `NARROWED_VERSIONED_REFS` gain `AgentTargetRef`.
- `test_metric_definition.py::_closed_enum_table`: new rows `LanguageDeclaration.source`, `MetricResult.language_source` (section 21.4), `VerdictCheck.outcome`, `WeightedExclusion.outcome`.

### 22.5 What batch 3 can turn green among the gate-bound tests

None of the four turns green yet; each gets closer. After an implementation of this section (checked on the oracle): `test_every_entity_has_registered_prefix` lacks the seven entities of P1-14 and P1-23 to P1-25 (`pcat`, `pce`, `use`, `cst`, `csum`, `rbnd`, `exp`; revision 15: six, `pce` withdrawn because `PriceEntry` became a value object, N47); `test_value_objects_have_no_entity_fields` lacks nine value objects (SealedPayload, RedactionRecord, UsageQuantity, BillingRules, PriceProvenance, PricingSnapshot, ReportedCostRaw, CostRounding, CostExclusion), and everything else it checks (the five typed references, the walks, `PerTurnSummary`, `CallerExternalId`) passes; `test_opaque_id_fields_are_exactly_the_adr_allowlist` has the set of opaque fields right (`KeyRef.key_id`, `ToolCallEvent.external_call_id`, `Run.caller_external_id`) and fails only on `SealedPayload.aad_fields.subject_id` (P1-26); `test_versioned_entity_set_matches_adr` lacks only `PricingCatalog` (P1-24). `test_every_id_reference_field_has_registered_prefix` stays green only if `EvidenceRef.ref_id` is the union of five ID classes (22.1).

### 22.6 What the tests do not cover (listed so it is not mistaken for coverage)

The bundle halves (P1-14): evidence resolution inside `RunBundle`, `declared_language_transcript_ids` resolving in a bundle, scorer `implementation_ref` equality across a bundle, the export rules and `export_human_subject`. The F1 to F8 mappings (P1-29 to P1-36), including `test_f8_untagged_transcript_language_reasons` and `test_f8_declared_language_reasons` (section 22.1 tests the same worked examples on synthetic records as `test_untagged_import_worked_example_6f` and `..._6h`). T-DEG (P1-19). The golden and frozen fixtures and the schema export (P1-15, P1-16). Real-time behaviour of the 50,000-turn test beyond its calibration (P-30): the limit scales with a pure-Python calibration loop measured in the test, with a reference of 0.05 s taken on the development machine (0.034 s).

### 22.7 Findings carried over

N11 (plan fields not adopted), N12 (code-only rules against gate item 2) and N17 to N19 stay open as listed in sections 19.6 and 21.5; N19 now also covers `LanguageDeclaration.declared_by` in `Transcript`, which the tests keep inside the transcript's `privacy` scope as ADR 0011 Decision 1a says.

### 22.8 Validation

Oracle: a correct implementation of this section outside the repository (`scratchpad/oracle10`), passing 240 of the 241 batch 3 tests on Python 3.11, 3.12, 3.13 and 3.14 (the 241st is the SCHEMA_POLICY.md test). 470 mutants of it were injected and run against the batch 3 tests; the result and the list of mutants not caught, each with the reason (equivalent in the oracle, or a reading the tests deliberately do not pin), are in `docs/phase1/evidence/red/batch3/mutants.txt`.

### 22.9 Test changes for qa: final list after revision 13 (schema-architect, 2026-10-23)

This is the one final list. Items 1 to 11 come from revision 11, items 12 to 21 from revision 12, and revision 13 made every item unconditional: the owner decided O3a, O8, O9 and N41 (option A) and confirmed the O1 and O5 details. No item is conditional, and no test was edited by the architect. Each item does one of three things: adds a test, strengthens a test, or corrects the document a test builds when an owner decision made that document invalid, keeping every assertion. No item weakens, skips or removes a check. "New test" marks a name that does not exist yet; SCOPE section 5 lists every one of them under its task, binding.

1. **N26, `test_snapshot.py::test_pricing_catalog_ref_digest_verified`.** Use the key `content_digest` instead of `digest` in `base`, in the bad cases (`None`, missing, upper-case hex) and in `keyed_ref`; read `reference.content_digest.value`; assert `set(ref_model.model_fields) == {"id", "version", "content_digest"}`; replace the unknown-field case `{**base, "content_digest": PLAIN}` by `{**base, "digest": PLAIN}` (the old name is now an unknown field and must be rejected); update the docstring (`PricingCatalogRef{id, version, content_digest}`) and the module docstring line 10. `snapshot_doc(..., pricing_catalog=base)` follows `base`.
2. **N27, `test_artifact.py::test_audio_format_fields`.** For `bits_per_sample` only, stop listing `None` as a rejected value (keep `0`, `-1`, `1.5`, `"16"`, `True`); the docstring names the field as optional. New test `test_audio_format_bits_per_sample_optional` (P1-10a): a lossy format (for example container `ogg`, codec `opus`) without the field validates and round-trips; an explicit `null` loads as absent; `bits_per_sample` is not in the JSON Schema `required` list while `container`, `codec`, `sample_rate_hz` and `channels` are; F6's 16-bit PCM keeps its value. (Removing `None` from the rejected values follows the N27 default: an absent bit depth is the truthful value for a lossy codec, so rejecting it would force a fabricated number.)
3. **N20, the digest helpers.** `_support_b2.seal()` must compute an Evaluator's digest over the Evaluator domain: remove `test_profile.content_digest` (that path only) before `compute_digest`, written out in the helper rather than by calling the implementation. The same change applies to `test_snapshot.py::_reseal_evaluator` (`_reseal_binding` reuses it; a MetricBinding has no `test_profile`, so removing that path changes nothing there, but the helper reads more plainly if the two are split). Builders in `_support_b3.py` that make an Evaluator with a profile digest inherit the fix through `seal`. Without this, an Evaluator built with a profile digest (for example `test_evaluator.py` near line 169, and `snapshot_parts(with_profile=True, profile_digest=PLAIN)`) fails its own digest check once the implementation follows N20. New test `test_evaluator_digest_excludes_test_profile_digest` (P1-09, P1-12): the Evaluator digest is unchanged when only `test_profile.content_digest` is changed, dropped or keyed; it changes when `test_profile.id` or `version` changes and when any other field changes; `evaluator.evaluator_digest_domain(doc)` removes only that path (a `content_digest` key inside `metadata` stays); a ConfigSnapshot whose evaluator's profile digest was dropped or keyed still loads, and `compute_snapshot_digest` gives the same value as with the plain digest. The P1-14 test `test_shared_export_snapshot_digest_verifies_after_profile_digest_dropped`, when written, edits the profile digest without resealing anything.
4. **N23.** New test `test_agent_channel_unknown_only_when_agent_audio_alone_is_missing` (P1-11): the five worked examples of ADR 0004 Decision 5a through `applicability`, each with its reason, its `missing_kinds` and `language_source=None`.
5. **N29.** New test `test_check_language_without_turns_or_transcripts` (P1-11): a language-dependent definition gives `("language_not_observed", None, [])`; a language-independent one gives `(None, None, [])`.
6. **N28.** New test `test_transcript_provider_call_ref_is_external_ref` (P1-10b): `provider_call_ref` is optional, accepts an `ExternalRef` object, round-trips it as an object, and rejects a bare string and a typed ID.
7. **N36 (resolved part).** New test `test_channel_map_channels_nonempty` (P1-10a): an empty `channels` list is rejected by pydantic and by JSON Schema (`minItems: 1`).
8. **N31 (resolved part).** `test_project_agent.py::test_hide_input_set_closed_under_embedding`: the seeds gain `Transcript`, `Turn`, `Segment`, `ToolCallEvent` and `IngestWarning` (meaning extended, name kept; items 15 and 17 add more seeds). New test `test_transcript_turn_and_tool_call_errors_do_not_echo_text` (P1-10b, P1-10c): a rejected Turn, Segment, ToolCallEvent and IngestWarning document whose text holds a marker string does not show it in `str(error)`, while the error still names the field.
9. **N34.** Confirm that `test_definition_refs_digest_optional_and_verified_when_present` covers both halves named in SCOPE: definition references outside a snapshot (Evaluator `persona` and `metric_bindings`, `MetricBinding.metric`, `RubricItem.binding`) and the snapshot's references. If it covers only the snapshot, extend it (meaning extended, name kept).
10. **N40.** When the T-DRIFT tests are written, the literal allowlist of `test_tdrift_allowlist_matches_adr` holds `packages/evalschema/tests/test_results_language.py` and `packages/evalschema/tests/test_dataset.py` (ADR 0005 Decision 10).
11. **P-62.** `test_judge_reported_model_verbatim_and_optional` and `test_judge_per_turn_reported_model_verbatim_and_optional` are binding under P1-11 (no edit; they were written in batch 3).
12. **O1 (decided in revision 12; the `None` detail confirmed by the owner in revision 13).** `test_privacy.py::test_human_subject_derived_from_caller_origin_human`: line 230, `derive_human_subject(human)` with the comment "the turns argument is optional", becomes `derive_human_subject(human, ())`, with the comment removed; the assertion (`is True`) is unchanged. The module docstring (lines 9 and 10) drops `=()` from both signatures. New test `test_human_subject_turns_argument_required` (P1-27): `derive_human_subject(run)` and `check_human_subject_marker(marker, run)` raise `TypeError`; `turns=None` raises `TypeError` for both; `inspect.signature(...).parameters["turns"].default is inspect.Parameter.empty` for both; an explicit `()` and `[]` on a simulated run without channel maps derive `True` (already pinned by lines 253 and 254, kept). New test at P1-14 `test_export_human_subject_turns_argument_required`: the same checks for `export_human_subject(marker, run, turns)`. `test_dataset.py` lines 160 and 162 already pass `turns` and stay. (Line 230 changes because the owner removed the default; the asserted value is unchanged.)
13. **O2.** `test_metric_definition.py::_closed_enum_table`: new row `("ToolCallEvent", ("source",), {"agent_export", "agent_telemetry", "observer"})` (meaning of `test_all_closed_enums_exact` extended, name kept). New test `test_tool_call_event_source_closed_set` (P1-10c): each of the three members validates and round-trips through JSON and JSON Schema; rejected by pydantic (error at `source`) and by JSON Schema: `telemetry`, `otel`, `webhook`, `Agent_Telemetry`, `agent-telemetry`, ` agent_telemetry`, `our_stt`, `script`, `human_reference`, `unknown`, `""`; the property's schema is `{"type": "string", "enum": [...]}`, never `const`. `test_events.py::test_tool_call_event_shape` keeps `_reject_call("source", source=None)`; its docstring may name the set. The `_support_b3.py` builders use `agent_telemetry` and stay valid.
14. **O3 and O3a (R1 on the Run, R2 over a bundle; O3a decided by the owner in revision 13).**
    - `_closed_enum_table`: new row `("Run", ("observed_tier",), {"T0", "T1", "T2"})`. The existing `test_run.py` checks (tier accepted at lines 99 to 101, required at line 134, bad spellings rejected at line 143) stay.
    - New test `test_observed_tier_t0_rejected_with_spans` (P1-12, R1): a Run with `"spans"` in `available_inputs` and `observed_tier="T0"` is rejected through `model_validate` and `model_validate_json`, error located at `observed_tier`, the message naming the rule and not echoing input; the same document with `T1` or `T2` validates; `T0` without `spans` validates; `T1` and `T2` without `spans` validate (an over-claim is not detectable on the record); `T0` with `detected_capabilities=["spans"]` and no `spans` in `available_inputs` validates (R1 does not read `detected_capabilities`). Its JSON Schema half follows the staff-reviewer's answer to N38 (an `if`/`then` with `contains: {"enum": ["spans"]}`, or a `KNOWN_SCHEMA_GAPS` entry).
    - R1 makes three existing test documents invalid. Each builds a Run with `spans` in `available_inputs` at the `_support_b3.run_doc` default `observed_tier="T0"`. The fix moves each document to the nearest valid record and keeps every assertion:
      - `test_results.py::test_available_inputs_not_derived_from_tier`, line 655 (`with_spans = _run(observed_tier="T0", available_inputs=[*evidence, "spans"])`): build `with_spans` for `T1` and for `T2` in a loop and keep both assertions for each. The loop at lines 646 to 654 has no `spans` in `available_inputs`, stays valid at all three tiers, and is unchanged.
      - `test_results.py::test_missing_inputs_helper`, lines 624 (`_run(available_inputs=["audio", "spans"])`) and 631 (`_run(available_inputs=["usage", "spans", "turn_timing"])`): pass `observed_tier="T1"`. The other calls of the test have no `spans` and stay at the default.
      - `test_results.py::test_na_missing_kinds_equal_requires_minus_available` (hypothesis): the strategy may put `"spans"` in `available`, so `_run(available_inputs=available)` becomes `_run(available_inputs=available, observed_tier="T1" if "spans" in available else "T0")`. The strategies, `max_examples`, `derandomize` and the assertions are unchanged, and T0 stays covered wherever it is valid.
      - Why this is a spec change and not a weakening: these three tests assert properties that do not depend on the tier (ADR 0004 Decision 2: no logic reads `observed_tier`; `missing_inputs` reads only `requires` and `available_inputs`). Under R1 their T0 documents with spans are invalid records, so as written they would fail at construction for a reason unrelated to what they test. Moving them to `T1` asserts the same property on a valid record. No assertion, case or example is removed. The rule itself is pinned by the new R1 test above.
      - A search of the test tree (`"spans"` and `available_inputs`) found no other Run built with `spans` available. The `_support_b3.run_doc` default (`T0`, `["audio", "transcript"]`) stays valid.
    - New test `test_bundle_observed_tier_equals_evidence_tier` (P1-14, R2): over a RunBundle, `check_observed_tier` accepts the stored tier only when it equals the tier computed from the provenance fields listed in ADR 0004 Decision 2a. Cases: one per field for T1 and one per field for T2; a T0 bundle with only `our_stt`, `script` and `agent_export` provenance; an under-claim and an over-claim, each rejected; a bundle with both kinds of evidence computing `T2`; a run whose only observer evidence is spans computing `T1`. Messages name the stored and computed tiers and one evidence record ID, never a value.
    - New test `test_export_declared_withheld_records_and_observed_tier` (P1-14, R2 with the owner's withheld-records requirement; field names follow the P1-14 shape, constraints in 22.12 item D3): an ExportBundle that declares withheld records of a run is accepted with a stored tier above the tier computed from the records present only when the declaration names a record kind that can carry the missing evidence. The same bundle without the declaration is rejected. An under-claim is rejected with or without a declaration. A declaration never raises the computed tier. Declaring only a kind without provenance fields (for example `Artifact`) does not excuse an over-claim. The declaration and the messages name record kinds and IDs, never values.
15. **O4.** `test_project_agent.py::test_hide_input_set_closed_under_embedding`: the seeds gain `ConfigSnapshot` and `Evaluator` (meaning extended, name kept; the docstring names them). The walk then also requires every model embedding them to hide (the P1-14 bundles when they exist). New test `test_config_snapshot_and_evaluator_errors_do_not_echo_input` (P1-12, also P1-09): a rejected Evaluator document whose free text holds a marker string does not show the marker in `str(error)`, `repr(error)`, `errors(include_input=False)` or `json(include_input=False)`, through `model_validate` and `model_validate_json`. Two seeds: a malformed placeholder in `expected_outcome` or a scripted turn, with the marker in the surrounding text of the same field; and an unknown field holding the marker, which is rejected as `extra_forbidden` and carries the marker only as `input_value`. The same evaluator embedded in a ConfigSnapshot document behaves the same. Each error still names the field location and the rule. This turns the batch 2 `Evaluator` red (it has no `hide_input_in_errors` today), and the P1-09 code then fixes it.
16. **O5 and O8 (O5 decided in revision 12, its details confirmed and O8 decided by the owner in revision 13).** `test_artifact.py`: existing cases stay (the relative `recordings/1.wav` and `/data/recordings/1.wav`, upper-case scheme, white space, bad escapes, userinfo, credentials). New tests:
    - `test_artifact_uri_scheme_set_closed` (P1-10a): accepted `http://h/1.wav`, `https://h/1.wav`, `s3://bucket/1.wav`, `gs://bucket/1.wav`, `file:///data/recordings/1.wav`; rejected (error at `uri`) `ftp://h/1.wav`, `data:audio/wav;base64,AAAA`, `blob:https://h/1`, `ws://h/1.wav`, `wss://h/1.wav`, `sip:rec@h`, `az://c/1.wav`, `hdfs://h/1.wav`, `//h/1.wav`, `./1.wav`, `s3:///1.wav` (no bucket), `https:///1.wav` (no host).

    - `test_artifact_uri_loopback_host_rules` (P1-10a, O8, record half). Records accept `http://localhost:9000/1.wav`, `https://LOCALHOST/1.wav`, `http://localhost./1.wav`, `http://rec.localhost/1.wav`, `http://127.0.0.1/1.wav`, `http://127.9.9.9:8080/1.wav`, `http://127.1/1.wav`, `http://[::1]/1.wav`, `http://[0:0:0:0:0:0:0:1]:9000/1.wav` and `http://[::ffff:127.0.0.1]/1.wav`, each round-tripping unchanged. The pure function `artifact.is_loopback_uri(uri)` (22.12 item D1) returns true for each of them. It returns false for `https://example.com/1.wav`, `http://localhost.example/1.wav`, `http://mylocalhost/1.wav`, `http://127.0.0.1.example/1.wav`, `http://128.0.0.1/1.wav`, `http://0.0.0.0/1.wav`, `http://[::]/1.wav`, `http://[::2]/1.wav`, `http://10.0.0.1/1.wav`, `s3://localhost/1.wav` (a bucket name, not a host) and `file:///data/1.wav`. No test may reject a loopback host on the record.
    - `test_shared_and_benchmark_exports_reject_file_uris` (P1-14): an ExportBundle with purpose `shared_export` or `benchmark` holding an Artifact with a `file:` URI is rejected, naming the artifact ID and not the path; `internal` accepts it.
    - `test_shared_and_benchmark_exports_reject_loopback_uris` (P1-14, O8, export half): the same three purposes with an Artifact whose `http`/`https` URI has a loopback host (one case per spelling class of the record half). `shared_export` and `benchmark` reject it, and the message names the artifact ID and the rule, never the URI. `internal` accepts it. The same bundles with `https://example.com/1.wav` are accepted by all three.
17. **N41 (decided by the owner in revision 13).** The seeds of `test_hide_input_set_closed_under_embedding` also gain `ScriptedTurn`, `ExpectedToolCall`, `Persona`, `MetricDefinition`, `MetricBinding` and `Rubric` (meaning extended, name kept). New test `test_definition_models_errors_do_not_echo_input` (P1-09; also P1-07, P1-08 and the Rubric of P1-22). For each of the six models validated on its own, `model_config["hide_input_in_errors"] is True`. A rejected document holding a marker string does not show the marker in `str(error)`, `repr(error)`, `errors(include_input=False)` or `json(include_input=False)`, through `model_validate` and `model_validate_json`, and the error is still located at the field. Seeds:

So the new test is red now and is turned green by the flag alone. `Rubric` and `MetricBinding` have no free-text field that the model rejects on its own, so seed (a) is their meaningful case. Seeds on identifier, key, closed-set or decimal fields are not used, because their messages quote the value (finding N42, 22.12).

No test asserts that one of these models echoes its input. The assertions that read error text read the message, which `hide_input_in_errors` does not change:

Both runs gave 17 failed and 201 passed, with the identical set of failures: the batch 3 snapshot tests, the gate-bound `test_ids.py` tests and the two `SCHEMA_POLICY.md` tests, all red before. The plugin was not saved in the repository.

These are new assertions of a decided rule, written red first; they replace no assertion.

### 22.10 Revision 11: needs owner decision (one at a time, in chat). Revision 12: all seven decided by the owner, option A

Each item is a genuine design choice that no ADR or core rule settles. The pinned reading of 22.1 stands until the owner answers; the tests already accept the recommended answer unless the item says otherwise. **Revision 12:** the owner decided O1 to O7 in chat, one at a time, each as recommended (option A); the decision log records them under "Batch 3 decisions". The questions are kept as asked; each ends with its decision.

- **O1 (N24 detail). Must `turns` be passed explicitly?** The tests call `derive_human_subject(run)` without turns and rely on the default `()`. Trade-off: with the default, a caller that forgets the turns of a transcript holding an `unknown` turn, on a simulated run whose channel map shows only caller and agent, gets `false`, a silent "not human" that the owner ruled out; with no default, every call states its turn evidence, and an empty sequence is an explicit "no turns". Cost: one test line and one new test. Recommendation: no default, for all three functions. **Decided (rev 12, owner): option A.** `turns` required on all three; omitted is a `TypeError`; explicit empty means no role evidence, so true (ADR 0016 Decision 5; 22.9 item 12). **Revision 13:** the owner confirmed the detail that `turns=None` also raises `TypeError` (only a real sequence is accepted).
- **O2 (N28). Is `ToolCallEvent.source` a closed set?** Trade-off: a closed set `agent_export | agent_telemetry | observer` (the spellings and meanings of `Transcript.source`, minus the producers that cannot observe a tool call) keeps tool-call provenance comparable and stops `telemetry`, `Telemetry` and `otel` from splitting one meaning, but an adapter for a new kind of producer then needs a schema change (a visible gap, ADR 0003 Consequences). An open `MachineKey` never blocks an adapter but cannot be relied on by any rule. Recommendation: the closed set, added to ADR 0003 Decision 1; the batch 3 tests use `agent_telemetry` and stay valid. **Decided (rev 12, owner): option A.** Closed `agent_export | agent_telemetry | observer` (ADR 0003 Decision 1, ADR 0012 Decision 1; 22.9 item 13).
- **O3 (N30). What is `Run.observed_tier`?** Trade-off: a closed, required `T0 | T1 | T2` (as the tests pin) is simple to display but needs a rule for a run with both telemetry and observer evidence; an optional field lets producers skip it, so displays would show gaps; dropping the stored field and computing a display value from evidence removes a second source of truth, but `available_inputs` does not say whether evidence came from telemetry or an observer, so it cannot be computed from what is stored. Recommendation: closed and required, added to ADR 0003 Decision 1, meaning "the highest tier whose evidence this run holds" (T2 if observer evidence, else T1 if telemetry evidence, else T0); still read by no logic (ADR 0004 Decision 2). **Decided (rev 12, owner): option A,** with the added clause that a check rejects values contradicting the recorded capabilities (ADR 0004 Decision 2a; 22.9 item 14). The capability fields cannot express every contradiction; the proposed rules are O3a (22.11).
- **O4 (N31 rest). Does `ConfigSnapshot` hide its input in errors?** Trade-off: by design a snapshot holds no personal data (placeholders, a zero `x-pii` walk), but user-typed text the lint cannot see may hold some (ADR 0006 Consequences), and a snapshot is copied into every bundle and export. Hiding protects that case at the cost of error texts that show the rule and location but not the rejected value, which makes snapshot authoring errors slower to debug. Recommendation: hide on `ConfigSnapshot` and on `Evaluator` (which carries the free text, so the set stays closed under embedding); messages keep naming field and rule. **Decided (rev 12, owner): option A** (ADR 0007 Decision 8 (b); 22.9 item 15; related item N41, 22.11, decided by the owner in revision 13).
- **O5 (N36). Which `Artifact.uri` schemes, and `file:` and fragments?** Trade-off: an open scheme set (any lowercase scheme with a host) never blocks a new store but accepts `data:` URIs that embed the recording itself in the record, and typos; a closed set needs a schema change per store kind, though most stores use `https`. Local CLI and CI runs (SQLite) keep recordings as local files, which `file:///abs/path` names but whose host is empty and whose path can hold a user name. Recommendation: the closed set `http`, `https`, `s3`, `gs`, `file`; `file:` only as `file:///` plus an absolute path; P1-14 rejects `file:` URIs in shared and benchmark exports (meaningless to a recipient, and the path may hold a user name); fragments rejected, as for ws and webrtc endpoints, since a range belongs in `EvidenceRef{kind: artifact_range}`. **Decided (rev 12, owner): option A,** with relative paths and `localhost` rejected (ADR 0007 Decision 6c; 22.9 item 16; loopback hosts in `http`/`https`: O8, 22.11). **Revision 13:** the owner confirmed the details exactly as pinned: `file:///` plus an absolute path only, `file:/path` rejected, no query, no `.` or `..` segments, no empty path, `file:///C:/...` accepted.
- **O6 (follow-on of N26). Rename `CostRecord.pricing_snapshot.digest` to `content_digest`?** Trade-off: one name for every catalog digest lets the bundle seam and readers treat them alike; the cost is a change to ADR 0014 Decision 12 and SCOPE before P1-25, where no test exists yet. Recommendation: rename, with the same rule as `PricingCatalogRef`. **Decided (rev 12, owner): option A** (ADR 0014 Decision 12; 22.9 item 18).
- **O7 (N22 wording). Confirm `DatasetItem.source_run_id`.** The owner's N22 decision reads "no run_id on records of fact except UsageRecord". `source_run_id` names the run a dataset item's recording came from (provenance; ADR 0016 Decision 5 uses it: an item without a source run has no role evidence), not membership of a run. Trade-off: without it, every recording item derives human-subject forever, even when it came from a simulated run whose roles are known. Recommendation: keep it, as pinned. **Decided (rev 12, owner): option A.** Kept as optional provenance only (not run membership); exports treat it as an unresolved reference, kept only where policy allows (ADR 0016 Decisions 5 and 9; 22.9 item 19; the policy is O9, 22.11, decided by the owner in revision 13).

Carried forward (not owner questions now): N33's open part (whether a RunBundle may carry a rescoring next to the original results) is decided at P1-14; N38 goes to staff-reviewer with N12; N11 and N17 to N19 stay open as listed in 22.7.

### 22.11 Revision 12: new owner items (one at a time, in chat). Revision 13: all four decided by the owner, option A

Applying O1 to O7 left four choices that the owner's words do not settle. Each has a trade-off and a recommendation. **Revision 13:** the owner decided all four in chat, one at a time, each as recommended (option A), and confirmed the O1 and O5 details (22.10); the decision log records them under "Batch 3 decisions". The questions are kept as asked; each ends with its decision. Every test that 22.9 named "conditional" in revision 12 is now binding (22.9 items 14, 16, 17 and 19). None of the four is open; gate item 11 has nothing left from this list.

- **O3a (from O3). The `observed_tier` contradiction rules** (ADR 0004 Decision 2a). The owner decided that a check rejects values contradicting the recorded capabilities. The capability fields (`available_inputs`, `detected_capabilities`) are `InputKind` lists, and an input kind does not say who produced it: transcripts, turn timing and tool calls can come from an agent export (T0), telemetry (T1) or an observer (T2). So no single rule over those fields covers the meaning "highest tier whose evidence the run holds". Proposed: **R1** (Run record, P1-12): `spans` in `available_inputs` with `observed_tier=T0` is rejected, because StageSpans carry trace or span IDs that only telemetry or an observer supply (premise to confirm: no T0 source supplies spans); `detected_capabilities` is not read, because the ADRs do not say whether a detection means this run holds the evidence. **R2** (RunBundle, P1-14): the stored tier must equal the tier computed from the closed provenance fields of the bundle's records (observer: `Transcript.source`, `Turn.timing_source`, `Exchange.source`, `TimelineOrigin.kind`, `ToolCallEvent.source` equal to `observer`; telemetry: the same fields at `agent_telemetry` or `telemetry`, any `StageSpan`, and agent-under-test `UsageRecord`s with `source=telemetry`). Trade-off: R1 alone catches only one under-claim and no over-claim; R2 catches both but needs the whole run, so a StageSpan produced by an observer counts only as telemetry (StageSpan has no producer field), and an ExportBundle that omits records cannot satisfy it (decided at P1-14). Without either rule the decided "check" has no content. Recommendation: accept R1 and R2. **Decided (rev 13, owner): option A, R1 and R2 accepted,** with the premise of R1 (no T0 source supplies spans) and the StageSpan limit of R2 as stated. Added by the owner: P1-14 must define how a bundle declares records deliberately withheld, so that R2 does not reject a legitimate partial export (constraints for P1-14 in 22.12 item D3). Tests: 22.9 item 14.
- **O8 (from O5). Loopback hosts in `http`/`https` artifact URIs.** The owner's `localhost` rule concerns `file:` URIs. `http://localhost:9000/bucket/1.wav` (a local object store in a CLI or CI setup), `127.0.0.1` and `[::1]` are not covered. Trade-off: rejecting them everywhere blocks a legitimate local setup; accepting them everywhere lets an address that means nothing to a recipient into exports, and it names the producer's own machine. Recommendation: accept on the record and in `internal` exports; reject in `shared_export` and `benchmark` exports, like `file:`. **Decided (rev 13, owner): option A.** Loopback hosts are allowed in `http`/`https` `Artifact.uri` values of ordinary records and `internal` exports and rejected in `shared_export` and `benchmark` exports (ADR 0007 Decision 6d; what counts as a loopback host: 22.12 item D1). Tests: 22.9 item 16.
- **O9 (from O7). Which export purposes keep an unresolved `DatasetItem.source_run_id`.** The owner said "kept only where policy allows" and no policy exists yet. Trade-off: keeping it everywhere preserves provenance for the producer's own tools, but in a shared or benchmark export an unresolved UUIDv7 gives the recipient nothing it can check, reveals its creation time and lets the recipient correlate items with the producer's internal runs; dropping it only makes the item's export-time derivation stricter (no role evidence, so true). Recommendation: `internal` keeps it; `shared_export` and `benchmark` keep it only when the source run is in the same bundle, otherwise omit it from the exported copy (the stored item is not edited). **Decided (rev 13, owner): option A.** Internal exports keep it; shared and benchmark exports keep it only if the source run is in the same bundle, and otherwise drop it with a recorded redaction (ADR 0016 Decision 9; the redaction entry: 22.12 item D2). Tests: 22.9 item 19.
- **N41 (from O4). Definition models validated on their own.** O4 covers `ConfigSnapshot` and `Evaluator`. `ScriptedTurn`, `ExpectedToolCall`, `Persona`, `MetricDefinition`, `MetricBinding` and `Rubric` can hold user-typed text and are validated on their own (registered models, authoring tools), where a rejected value is shown in the error. Inside a hiding model they are already protected, because the outermost setting governs. Trade-off: hiding protects the same free-text case O4 protects, at the same cost (error texts without the rejected value, slower authoring debugging). Recommendation: hide on all six. **Decided (rev 13, owner): option A.** All six hide their input in validation errors, closed under embedding (ADR 0007 Decision 8 (b)). The batch 2 code of the six gains `hide_input_in_errors=True`. No existing batch 2 test asserts echo behaviour, so none turns red except through the new seeds and the new test (22.9 item 17, with the evidence).

### 22.12 Revision 13: details fixed while applying the decisions, and new finding N42

Details fixed by the architect while applying O3a, O8 and O9. Each follows from the owner's words and an existing rule, and each is listed so the owner can see it. None is a [Proposed] item: if the owner objects to one, it changes before its task.

- **D1 (O8). What counts as a loopback host** (ADR 0007 Decision 6d). The rule applies only to `http` and `https` URIs; for `s3` and `gs` the authority is a bucket name, not a network host. The host is compared after ASCII case-folding and the removal of one trailing dot, both as written and percent-decoded once (the two texts of Decision 6b). It is a loopback host if any of these holds:
  - it is `localhost` or ends in `.localhost` (RFC 6761 section 6.3 reserves these names for loopback);
  - the WHATWG URL Standard IPv4 parser maps it into `127.0.0.0/8` (RFC 1122 section 3.2.1.3). This covers the shorthand, octal, hexadecimal and integer spellings that common HTTP clients resolve (`127.1`, `0x7f.0.0.1`, `2130706433`), so a spelling cannot slip a loopback address into a shared export;
  - it is an IPv6 literal equal to `::1` or to an IPv4-mapped address in `::ffff:127.0.0.0/104` (RFC 4291 sections 2.5.3 and 2.5.5.2), in any textual spelling.

  Not loopback, because the owner's decision names loopback only: the unspecified addresses `0.0.0.0` and `[::]`, private and link-local ranges (`10.0.0.0/8`, `192.168.0.0/16`, `fe80::/10`), and names such as `host.docker.internal`. Those hosts also mean nothing to a recipient; widening the export rule to them would be a new owner decision and is not assumed here. The rule is exposed as a pure function `artifact.is_loopback_uri(uri) -> bool` (P), so that P1-10a can test the definition before the P1-14 export check uses it.
- **D2 (O9). The recorded redaction of an omitted `source_run_id`** (ADR 0016 Decision 9). The owner said "dropped with a recorded redaction". The existing `RedactionRecord{method, tool_ref, performed_at, covers[]}` asserts that a tool redacted a payload's content (audio, text, tool arguments), and `covers[]` lists payload kinds. Omitting a reference field is a different act. P1-14 pins the shape and must keep these properties:
  - the entry names the record ID (the DatasetItem), the field (`source_run_id`) and a reason (`unresolved_reference`), and never the omitted value;
  - it is serialized in the ExportBundle, so a recipient can see that the field was removed;
  - it never counts as the seal-or-redaction of a human-subject payload (ADR 0016 Decision 7);
  - export validation rejects a `shared_export` or `benchmark` bundle that still carries an unresolved `source_run_id`, and rejects an entry whose item is absent or still carries the field.

  Whether the entry is a new `RedactionRecord` form or a separate value object is decided with the P1-14 shape. If that choice changes an accepted decision, it goes to the owner first.
- **D3 (O3a). Declared withheld records and R2** (ADR 0004 Decision 2a). The owner requires P1-14 to define how a bundle declares records deliberately withheld. Constraints the P1-14 shape must meet:
  - the declaration is explicit, per run, and names record kinds and IDs, never values;
  - R2 without a declaration is strict;
  - with a declaration, an under-claim (stored tier below the tier computed from the records present) is always rejected, because withholding cannot remove evidence that is present;
  - an over-claim is accepted only when the declaration covers a record kind that can carry the missing evidence (the kinds with provenance fields in Decision 2a: Transcript, Turn, Exchange, ToolCallEvent, StageSpan, UsageRecord); a declared Artifact alone does not excuse an over-claim;
  - a declaration never raises the computed tier;
  - P1-14 states whether a record replaced by a `SealedPayload` keeps its provenance fields readable or must be declared withheld, and whether a redacted record keeps its provenance fields (redaction covers payload content).

  Whether a RunBundle, the unit of a run (N22), may declare withheld records at all, or only an ExportBundle may, is part of the same P1-14 definition.

New finding (revision 13, for the owner; not decided here):

- **N42. Messages that quote the rejected value inside hiding models.** `hide_input_in_errors=True` removes the `input_value` part of a pydantic error and nothing else. Several shared validators of batches 1 and 2 put the rejected value into the message text itself:
  - the typed-ID check ("'...' is not a valid ID of this kind");
  - the `MachineKey`, `MetricKey` and `implementation_ref` patterns;
  - `ClosedSet` ("'...' is not one of (...)");
  - the BCP-47 tag check;
  - the `DecimalString` check (for example a Rubric weight);
  - pydantic's own discriminator message ("Input tag '...' found using ...").

  The architect checked these on the current batch 2 models (Persona, MetricDefinition, MetricBinding, Rubric and Evaluator, with the flag set on scratch copies). A value pasted into such a field still appears in `str(error)` with the flag set. The batch 3 hiding models (Run, Turn, Artifact and the others of Decision 8 (b)) reuse the same validators. ADR 0007 Decision 8 (c) forbids value-quoting messages only for credential and personal-data fields, so this is within the decided text. It is still a gap against the purpose of hiding, because a user can paste anything into any field.
  - Trade-off: removing the quoted value from every message of a hiding model closes the gap, but authoring errors such as a misspelled `kind` would no longer show the typo; the message would show the rule and the allowed form only. Existing tests that assert a message quotes a value would need review. Keeping the current rule leaves identifier and closed-set fields quoting their value.
  - Recommendation: extend Decision 8 (c) to every field of a hiding model (name the rule and the expected form, never the value). The new no-echo tests of 22.9 items 15 and 17 hold under either answer, because they place the marker only in free text and in unknown fields.
  - Decide before the batch 3 implementation starts (P1-10a), because the batch 3 hiding models reuse these validators.
  - **Decided (revision 14, owner): option A**, with two refinements decided by the owner: the `DecimalString` canonical hint stays outside hiding models, and inside them the unknown-profile-placeholder message names the rule, not the typed field. Applied in ADR 0007 Decision 8 (c) and I15, and ADR 0006 Decision 9a; implementation in 22.13.

### 22.13 Revision 14: N42 decided, batch 3 implemented (schema-architect, 2026-10-23)

**Owner decisions applied** (decision log "Batch 3 decisions"): N42 option A (ADR 0007 Decision 8 (c) covers every field of a hiding model); refinement 1 (the `DecimalString` hint stays for bare `TypeAdapter` and free-standing use); refinement 2 (inside hiding models the unknown-profile-placeholder message gives the location and the rule, not the typed field name); the loopback spellings qa pinned in `test_artifact_uri_loopback_host_rules` (ADR 0007 Decision 6d).

**How N42 is implemented.** Every hiding model derives from the private base `tracer_evalschema._hiding.HidingModel` (`hide_input_in_errors=True`). Each subclass gets a wrap model validator, attached as its outermost validator, that sets a context flag (`_hiding.values_hidden()`) for its whole validation, embedded models included; so the outermost hiding model governs, as for `hide_input_in_errors`. The shared validators read the flag and then put neither the value nor a part of it in the message or in the error context: typed IDs, `FullMatch` patterns (`MachineKey`, `MetricKey`, `implementation_ref`, digest values, `sha256`, `declared_by`), `ClosedSet`, `ObservedLanguageTag` and `SupportedLanguage` (no subtag either; the code-mix constant stays, it quotes no input), `DecimalString` (no canonical spelling either), `UtcDatetime`, the currency, the URL rules (no parameter name), list-duplicate messages, `check_binding_params` and `validate_rubric` keys when called inside a snapshot, and `UnknownDigestScheme`. The discriminated unions (`PassRule`, `Aggregation`, `Endpoint`, `TimelineOrigin.ref`) use a custom union error that names the allowed tags, always. Model-level messages of hiding models never quote values. The registry walks public modules only, so `HidingModel` is not a schema model.

**Batch 2 code changed:** `Evaluator`, `ScriptedTurn`, `ExpectedToolCall`, `Persona`, `MetricDefinition`, `MetricBinding`, `Rubric` hide (O4, N41); `Evaluator.content_digest` is computed over `evaluator.evaluator_digest_domain` (N20) through the new hook `VersionedEntity.digest_subject()`; `ProviderConfigRef`, `Project`, `AgentTarget`, the endpoints and `TestProfile` derive from `HidingModel`; `AgentTargetRef` is in `agent`.

**Public names added beyond 22.1** (not pinned by tests; listed so that the schema export and staff-reviewer see them): `base.verify_entity_digest` (the one entry point for an entity's own digest, also used by `ConfigSnapshot`); `language.SupportedLanguageNoScript`, `SUPPORTED_LANGUAGE_NO_SCRIPT_PATTERN`, `script_subtag`, `allowed_scripts`; `url_rules.check_artifact_uri`, `is_loopback_host_uri`, `ARTIFACT_URI_SCHEMES`, `ARTIFACT_URI_PATTERN`; `artifact.Confidence`; `privacy.role_evidence`; `events.EvidenceId` and `EVIDENCE_ID_PATTERN`; the closed-set constants of each module; `snapshot.EXPECTED_OUTCOME_VARIABLE`.

**Details fixed while implementing** (each follows from the pinned contract; listed, not guessed, for staff-reviewer):
1. Segment bounds (P-28): every present segment bound lies within the turn's present bounds (start and end), not only the same-named bound. When all four bounds are present the two readings agree; they differ only when a bound is missing (a segment that ends before the turn starts is rejected).
2. `ToolCallEvent`: no rule that `arguments` equals the parse of `arguments_raw` when both are present (not pinned; a producer may legitimately supply both, and comparing would need a number-equality rule).
3. Integer fields of the new models (`Artifact.size`, counts, indexes, `seq`) are bounded by the I-JSON range (+-(2**53 - 1)), like the result value.
4. `s3`/`gs` artifact URIs use the generic authority grammar of the http rules (host and optional port); bucket-name syntax is not checked.
5. Loopback: userinfo is ignored and the scheme compared case-insensitively in `is_loopback_uri` (mutants LB24 and LB30, not pinned); a zone identifier in an IPv6 literal is not loopback; after the one trailing dot is removed, a remaining trailing dot is an empty label and not loopback (`127.0.0.1..`, like `localhost..`). Not covered: a non-ASCII host after percent-decoding (the WHATWG host parser maps it through UTS 46, for example full-width letters; D1 says ASCII case-folding only, so no mapping is applied).
6. `file:` paths: dot segments are compared as written (`%2e%2e` is not a dot segment) and an empty segment (`//`) is accepted (owner: left to staff-reviewer).
7. `check_language` with turns reads only the transcripts the turns name; with no turns it reads every supplied transcript.
8. `MetricResult.value` is required (nullable): it is not marked optional in 22.1.
9. `ConfigSnapshot` does not require every embedded binding to be referenced by the evaluator or every embedded definition to be bound (not pinned).
10. `check_verdict` compares a check's `metric_ref` digest with the binding's only when both are present and comparable (same scheme and `key_ref`), as E5.
11. JSON Schema `if`/`then` presence rules are emitted for `PrivacyMarker` (P-46), `ChannelMap` (confidence by determination), `Turn` (`timing_source=none`), `ToolCallEvent` (`arguments` with `arguments_complete=false`) and `StageSpan` (`stage_raw`), as the architect recommended for N38; the arithmetic, cross-record and digest rules stay code-only. N12/N38 are still open with staff-reviewer.
12. Hiding models of batch 3 are exactly those pinned (`Artifact`, `Transcript`, `Turn`, `Segment`, `ToolCallEvent`, `IngestWarning`, `Run`, `CallerExternalId`, `ConfigSnapshot`); `Exchange`, `StageSpan`, `MetricResult`, `RunVerdict`, `Dataset` and `DatasetItem` do not hide (not pinned).

**Tests that conflict with refinement 2** (green before, red now; not edited): `test_evaluator.py::test_profile_placeholder_unknown_field_rejected` (`match=unknown`, ScriptedTurn), `::test_malformed_profile_placeholder_rejected` (`match="shoe_size"`, ScriptedTurn), `::test_profile_placeholders_in_outcome_and_tool_arguments` (`match=unknown`, ExpectedToolCall) and `test_persona_profile.py::test_every_profile_field_is_usable_as_a_placeholder` (`"unknown_field" in str(caught)`, Evaluator). Each asserts that the message names the typed field on a model that hides its input since N41 (revision 13). Under the owner's decision these assertions match on the rule instead (the message contains "unknown profile placeholder field" and the known fields). The rejection itself, its location and every other assertion of these tests still hold.

**Not applied:** the owner's later live-monitoring direction (a `live` run mode) is outside batch 3; `Run.mode` stays `simulated | recorded` as pinned.

## 23. Batch 4 (tasks P1-23, P1-24, P1-25): usage, pricing and cost; API assumed by the tests

Owner: qa (2026-10-23, against SCOPE revision 13). Audience: schema-architect (implements exactly this), staff-reviewer. Tests: `test_usage.py` (P1-23), `test_pricing.py` (P1-24), `test_cost.py`, `test_cost_summary.py`, `test_cost_compute.py`, `test_cost_import.py` (P1-25), `test_cost_contract.py` (all three, schema and float contract), `test_batch4_entities.py` (the five entities); helper `_support_b4.py`. Status: written and red; nothing in `src/` was written by qa. A throwaway oracle (outside the repository, built on the batch 1 to 3 oracle) passes all 206 collected tests on Python 3.11 to 3.14, and 228 injected mutants were run against it (23.5). If a name or field below is wrong for a good reason, escalate to staff-reviewer; do not change a test.

The owner's decisions are treated as binding: USD only (ADR 0014 Decisions 1 to 5), `basis` is `reported|computed` only, `computed` needs known usage and a known price with billing rules, a black-box agent's cost is `not_available`, a reported non-USD or currency-less cost is `not_available` with the raw values kept, the label rules of P-52 (`usd`, ` USD ` normalized with a warning, `$` and `US$` never mapped), the P-54 one-usage-one-cost rule, O6 (`pricing_snapshot.content_digest`), N26, and "never assume: a missing cost is never 0". Readings the tests pin where the ADRs leave a gap are marked (P) and listed in 23.3 as N43 and later. Conventions of sections 19 and 22 apply (`extra="forbid"`, closed sets emit `enum` and never `const`, rules across fields are reported at the field they are about, value objects carry no entity fields).

### 23.1 Modules, classes and pinned fields

**Revision 15 (2026-10-23, schema-architect):** the owner decided N43 and N46 to N60 (23.3).
